| 562 | B-99-b | **A function whose body reads a `comptime` parameter's `.value` is expanded at its call site: what depends on build data runs at build, what reaches a run-time value is emitted there as run-time code** The maintainer, 2026-10-15: (d), written with a plain `for` — the `comptime` parameter is the mark, no `comptime for`. **Only a function whose every parameter is `comptime` is expanded** (`comptime` interacts with `comptime` and with constants alone — never with a run-time variable): each argument at the call is known at build (417), a run-time variable passed is `comptime-arg-not-known`; a function that also declares a run-time parameter is never expanded — its `comptime` parameters are its specialisation (417), reading one's `.value` keeps it `Stage.Build` (376 (1)), and reaching a run-time value from it is refused `build-and-run` as before. The call is replaced by the code its body emits, written where the call is (for `rakun.boot()`, the program's `main`, in the root package — 551). In the body, a loop over a `comptime` parameter's `.value`, a condition, a `let` and a call answered from build data alone (a `Declared`'s `name`, `module`, `meta`, a literal, another `comptime` value, a constant — a `val` whose value reads no run-time variable; std's functions over them, 380) run at build, and `x.fail(…)` reports there; each statement that reaches a run-time value — a `Declared`'s `value`, a host call, a function of `Stage.Run` — is emitted, in order, once per iteration of the loop that holds it, the build-known parts written into it as constants (`log.info("starting ${d.name}"); d.value.make().start();` over three modules emits three pairs of calls in the modules' order). A loop's bound or a condition that needs a run-time value is refused at it, located, naming the value ("`d.value.make()` runs only at run time — this condition is decided at build"). The function itself has no run-time body: nothing iterates the catalogue at run time, and an entry's `value` is never called at build (356). Amends 376 (1): in a function whose every parameter is `comptime`, reading one's `.value` no longer makes it `Stage.Build`; it makes it expanded | `1-botopink-lang/144` B-99 · `2-libraries/165` s5, s7 | comptime_parameter_call_expanded |

| 570 | B-116-a | **Without a marker a body returns the whole type: an unmarked `-> Async<T>` / `-> Component<R>` body returning the bare value is a type error; the codemod marks today's such bodies** The maintainer, 2026-10-16: (a), the rule stated. The marker decides what a body's `return` takes: `async fn load() -> Async<i32> { return 1 }` returns the value, wrapped by the compiler; an unmarked `fn load() -> Async<i32>` is an ordinary function and returns an `Async<i32>` itself — `return Async.resolve(1)` — so `fn load() -> Async<i32> { return 1 }` is refused as a type mismatch (`i32` is no `Async<i32>`), the hint naming `async fn` and `Async.resolve`. The same for `Component<R>` and the `component` marker. A caller sees no difference (`await load()` either way). std gives `Async.resolve(v: T) -> Async<T>` (JS `Promise.resolve`, Rust `std::future::ready`), declared with the type in 553's piece (ii). The code written today is adjusted, never left to break: before the refusal is on (144 B-116 P4), the codemod (P2 over botopink-lang, P3 per library) adds `async` / `component` to every `-> @Task<T>` / `-> @Component<R>` body that compiles specially today without writing a keyword (a `@Task` body returning a value, most of jhonstart's and rakun's components), the body unchanged and every cell's answer the same | `1-botopink-lang/144` B-116 · `2-libraries/150` | unmarked_body_returns_whole_type |
| 575 | (the maintainer, 2026-10-16–17, case by case) | **No declaration files; a builtin is a std declaration marked `#[builtin]`; a `declare fn` names its host sources with `#[compiler.external(…)]`; a build-time body talks to the compiler through the keyword `compiler`; `comptime` and `syntax` on a parameter say how an argument arrives.** (1) **No `.d.bp`.** The declaration-file kind goes — `DECLARATION_EXT`, `Module.declaration`, every reader —; every `.d.bp` is renamed `.bp` or dissolved: `builtins.d.bp` and `builtins_fns.d.bp` into the std modules that own each declaration; rakun's `rakun.d.bp` (declares nothing) deleted with its `files` entry; the conformance cells' renamed (`files_one_module_twice` keeps its one-file-listed-twice form). A file still ending in `.d.bp` is a module named `x.d`, nothing special. Replaces 252, 514, 515; amends 66. (2) **`#[builtin]`.** `@name` is a std declaration marked `#[builtin]` — `pub fn builtin(syntax decl: unknown) -> void { if (!compiler.isStd) compiler.fail("…", at: decl); compiler.setBuiltin(true); }` —, each in the std module where it belongs (`Result` in `result.bp`, `embed` and `Asset` in `asset.bp` over `io.fs.File`, `src` and `typeInfo` in `reflection.bp`, `Compiler` in `compiler.bp`, `print` in `io.bp`, `panic` and `todo` in `panic.bp`); inside its own module the name is written without `@`; two std declarations marking one name `builtin-twice`, outside std `setBuiltin-outside-std`. (3) **`compiler`, a keyword.** In a body that runs at build — a decorator, a template, a function called at build, a `comptime` block — `compiler` is the `Compiler` of that point: the annotated declaration in a decorator, the call site elsewhere; a `Build` resource (376 (1)), `compiler-at-run-time` in a run-time-only body; never a parameter (`src()`, `embed(path)`, `typeInfo(t)` take none). `Compiler` is a `behavior` of std whose one implementor is the compiler — no `#[Intrinsic]`, no `Implementation.Compiler`; a unit test holds the compiler's table of its members to `compiler.bp` —: `src`, `module`, `package`, `isStd`; `fail(message, syntax at: ?unknown = null)` (at the argument or the declaration `at` holds, else at `src`; the body goes on); the outputs, a decorator's only (`compiler-output-outside-decorator`): `compile(syntax executor: fn())` — run-time code compiled beside the annotated declaration, run once before `main` in the program's init list: modules in dependency order, declarations in source order, calls in order; any number per declaration —, `setMeta<M>(syntax value: M)` (298, one per `M`), `addMember<F>(name, syntax member: F)` (370 (2)'s typed form, the only one: the string form goes), `addType(name, syntax source: type)`, `implement(source: Implementation)` (one per target, `implementation-twice`; on a declaration with a body `implement-with-body`), `setBuiltin(on)`; the reads: `embed(path) -> Bytes` (519's checks), `catalogue(with, member = null)` (253; its entries declaration handles, read at build, 576 (7)), `record(fields: compiler.Field<unknown>[]) -> type` (307: named by the module-level `val` that binds the answer, `derived-type-outside-val`, `derived-type-cycle`, `record-field-duplicate`; a field that `fields` produced keeps its source's default and markers, 423), `fields<T>(t: type<T>) -> compiler.Field<T>[]`, `optional<T>(f)` / `required<T>(f)` over a field (`?F` / `F`, source kept), `typeInfo<T>(t: type<T>) -> TypeInfo<T>`, `text(syntax e: unknown) -> string`, `name(syntax e: unknown) -> ?string` (`null` for an expression with no name, 643). Replaces 216's four places; amends 298. (4) **`comptime` and `syntax` on a parameter; no `Expr<T>` type.** `comptime x: T`: a value known at build, the body holds a `T`. `syntax x: T`: the expression written, captured as a tree, opaque in the body — handed only to `compiler.*` or into another tree; a decorator's first `syntax` is the annotated declaration, filled by the compiler and checked against the parameter's type (280 (2)). `-> syntax T`: a template's return, the return expression captured and spliced at the call (replaces `@expr`). An `syntax` argument to an `syntax` parameter hands its tree on. `@Expr<T>`, `.value` and `Decl<T>` go (reverts 364 (4); replaces 364 (1), (2) and 370 (1)); `Declared<T>` goes too (576 (7)). **The tree rule:** a tree — an executor, a meta value, a member, a template's return — holds exactly run-time code (names resolved where written, 384), `syntax` (spliced as the expression held) and `comptime` (parameters and the body's `val`s, spliced as literals); a `compiler.*` call inside one is evaluated at build and spliced as its answer (614; its argument a build value, else `compiler-arg-at-run-time`); a `-> syntax T` function is called only by the template syntax, an annotation, or inside a tree (`syntax-call-outside-syntax`). (5) **`type<T>`.** The type of the type `T`, the generic form of `type`: `comptime t: type<T>` takes a type and binds `T` (`@typeInfo(city)` with a value is refused); replaces `type T` at its seven sites; `type(` / `type {` stay the inline type; opaque, read through `compiler.fields` / `compiler.typeInfo`. (6) **A `declare fn` names its sources.** A bodyless `declare fn` — top-level or a type's, static included — is refused at the declaration, `declare-without-implementation`, unless an annotation gave it an implementation; a target without one is refused at the call as today (146; amends 311 (1)). `external` is one std function, `#[builtin] pub fn external(syntax decl: unknown, comptime ..sources: Implementation[]) -> void { for (s in sources) compiler.implement(s); }`, `Implementation` the enum `Erlang(template, inline = false, result = false) | JavaScript(template, result = false) (618, 619) | Wasm(template | op | std | wasi — one —, host, needs, result)`, written `#[@external(.Erlang("…"), .JavaScript("…"), .Wasm(std: "…"))]` (no `@` inside std); the shape errors are the enum constructor's; the compiler reads `Implementation` alone — `external_variants`, `translateExternalCells` and the backends' re-parse of the prelude go, the table checks (`fn:`, `op:`, `std:`, `wasi:`, `result:`, `host:` twice, 516) stay over the value —; a library writes its own source annotation the same way (`#[bif("lists")]` over `compiler.implement`). `#[@Host]`, `declare` inside a `behavior` (`declare-in-behavior`) and the delegate forms (`val x = declare fn`, the unannotated shorthand) go. `print`, `println` and `debug` are bound by their cells on every target (`.Wasm(std: "println")`); no backend intercepts them. Amends 238, 305, 481, 516, 518; 445 keeps `std:` std's. (7) **Bodies in std.** What the compiler lowered by name is written in botopink: `Result`'s five methods (`case` over `Ok` / `Error`; the backends' inline lowering and `comptime/builtins.zig`'s `methods` rows go); `src()` (`return compiler.src`); `embed(comptime path: string) -> Asset` (`compiler.embed`; `Asset(path, size, hash, file: File)`, `text()` = `self.file.text()`, run time on every target; `File.ofBytes` replaces `fs.openAsset`; `Asset.open()`, `embed-text-not-utf8` and `embed-text-not-at-build` go — amends 519, 538); `typeInfo(comptime t: type<T>)` (`compiler.typeInfo`); `TypeInfo.all` goes (576 (7)); `types.bp` whole: `compiler.Field<T>(name, type: type, annotations)` (`type` for `typeName`; a `type` value at run time is its name), `of` / `all` over `compiler.fields(T)` (computed at build, 380), `optional()` / `required()`, `keys` (`return Field<T>`), `partial` / `required` / `merge` / `pick` / `omit` over `compiler.fields` + `compiler.record` (`derived_types.zig` keeps only the record's materialisation and the two `val` rules; amends 307, 308). (8) **Removed — no use anywhere:** `@module()` (closes ck2-a; the cell `builtin_module_not_lowered` goes), `@trap()`, `@field`, `@TypeOf`, `@makeRecord` with `RecordField`, `@RecordKeys`, `@comptimeError`, `@compilerError` (`compiler.fail`), `@code`, `@expr`, `@emit` — `compiler.init` and the typed outputs replace it: rakun's ~60 `val __rk*` registrations, its 4 generated functions and 2 generated types migrate in 165 s10 before botopink-lang removes `emit`. (9) The cost of std's annotations evaluated on the comptime runtime per process is the analysis cache's (508–513), measured before the steps land. | `1-botopink-lang/144` B-136 … B-140 · `2-libraries/165` s10 | no_declaration_files_compiler_value |
| 576 | (the maintainer, 2026-10-17) | **`@Expr` and `@Decl` go whole; the template model hangs off `compiler`.** (1) The names `Expr<E>` (std's behavior), `@Expr<T>`, `Decl` / `Decl<T>` and `@Decl` are removed with their std declarations and every reader; after the codemod they are unknown types. The old parameter modifier of 1.0.10 goes with them (its meaning is the one modifier, named `syntax` by 616; no `.bp` wrote the old one). (2) Each member of `Expr<E>` becomes a `Compiler` member taking the `syntax` it read as its first argument: `compiler.text(e)` (575 (3)), `compiler.parts(e) -> Part[]`, `compiler.source(e) -> Source`, `compiler.context(e) -> ExprContext`, `compiler.lookup(e, name)`, `compiler.bindings(e)`, `compiler.build<R>(source) -> syntax R` (426 (1): `R` the call's type), `compiler.note(message)` (426 (4)), `compiler.fail(message, at: e, span: ?Span = null)` (`failAt` goes), `compiler.tagDecl(kind, name, attrs)`, `compiler.annotate(d, at: Span)` (302). (3) What answered a `Decl` answers an opaque declaration handle typed `unknown`, read only through `compiler.*`: `compiler.name(d)`, `compiler.kind(d) -> DeclKind`, `compiler.moduleOf(d)`, `compiler.meta<M>(d)` / `compiler.metaAll<M>(d)`, `compiler.attr(d, name)`, `compiler.component(d)`; the outputs on it stay refused (415 (2)). (4) `@ExprCustom<T>` goes: a template answers `-> syntax T` and attaches its reference tree with `compiler.custom(ast: CustomNode)`, stored by call location for the language server as today; 275's `R` is read from `-> syntax R`. (5) `Span`, `Part`, `Source`, `ExprContext` and `CustomNode` move to `compiler.bp`, their fields unchanged; `CustomNode.ref` holds a declaration handle. (6) The codemod rewrites `q.<m>(…)` → `compiler.<m>(q, …)`, `-> @ExprCustom<T>` → `-> syntax T` with `compiler.custom`, over std and `conformance/` in B-136, over the libraries in 165 s10. (7) **`Declared<T>` goes**, with `DeclaredMeta` and `DeclaredTypedMeta`: no catalogue is read at build or at run time (`compiler.catalogue` / `compiler.find` gone, 665) — `@TypeInfo.all` goes —, and a run-time registry is written by the markers themselves, each `compiler.init`-ing its declaration's registration into the init list (575 (3)): rakun-bpp's `#[page]`, `#[layout]`, `#[template]` and `#[defaultView]` register their function with its segment, and `jhRegisterRoutes` over `Declared<unknown>[]` goes (165 s10); the order a registry needs is its own. The hooks model's declaration fields (277) — `HookNode.fn`, `HookCall.callee`, `HookUse.context` — hold declaration handles. (8) **Every other read of a declaration** follows (3): `d.<r>` → `compiler.<r>(d)` — `hooks`, `fields` (the `compiler.Field` list `compiler.fields(T)` answers for a type), `methods`, `params`, `returnType`, `owner`, `annotations`, `variants` —, each answering what it answers today. (9) `compiler.addMeta<M>(syntax value: M)` and `compiler.wrapWith(syntax f)` (316's typing) are outputs beside 575 (3)'s, a decorator's only. (10) **A decorator is a function whose first parameter is `syntax`** (575 (2)'s `builtin(syntax decl: unknown)`). (11) **The string meta goes** (the maintainer, 2026-10-17): `setMeta(key, value)`, `.meta.<decorator>.<key>` and the string reads of `DeclaredMeta` are removed; a decorator records a typed meta record (`compiler.setMeta<M>(value)`, 298) and is read through `compiler.meta<M>(d)` / `metaAll<M>(d)` — one spelling (67); the sites migrate by hand, a meta record per decorator, std and `conformance/` in B-136, the libraries in 165 s10. (12) A meta record's field written `@Expr<T>` (370 (1)) is written `T`: it holds a tree when filled from an `syntax` (575 (4)'s tree rule), no field modifier. (13) **The types of `compiler.bp` are imported** (the maintainer, 2026-10-17): `Span`, `Part`, `Source`, `ExprContext`, `CustomNode`, `DeclKind` and the records `compiler.*` reads answer (`HookNode`, `Field`, `Method`, `Param`, `DeclAnnotation`) are no prelude names; a file naming one writes `import {compiler.Span} from "std";`; the codemod adds the imports. (14) The string `addMember(name, source)` and `addType(name, source)` stay until B-140 gives a type written as a value (575 (7)) and `compiler.record`: std's `#[mock]` writes `T.Mock(…)`, and a computed shape has no other spelling. (15) Order: B-136 builds `compiler.catalogue` at build (handles read through `compiler.*`); `Declared<T>`, `DeclaredMeta` and the run-time `@TypeInfo.all` go in B-137 with `compiler.init`, so no catalogue cell loses its run-time half between them. | 577 | 14s8-a · (the maintainer, 2026-10-17) | **A template is called one way: its name before a template text `` @`…` ``, raw.** (1) **The template text** `` @`…` `` is a token of its own: it may span lines, holds its bytes as written — no escape sequences but `` \` `` for a backtick, no interpolation — and is the only literal a template takes; `${…}` and `{…}` inside it are the sub-language's own syntax, parsed by the template from its raw text (14s8-a (e): a hole's expression is spliced into the code the template answers and typed in the caller's scope; the template never reads its value). (2) A `-> syntax T` function — a template — takes exactly that text, its first `syntax string` parameter (616); as an annotation it takes a second `syntax` parameter, the annotated declaration the compiler fills (576). (3) It is called only as `` name @`…` `` — in run-time code or inside a tree — or as an annotation `` #[name @`…`] ``; `name "…"`, `name """…"""`, `name(…)`, `name<T>(…)`, `#[name(…)]` and the template taken as a value are `syntax-call-outside-syntax` (narrows 575 (4)'s three places). (4) A `-> syntax T` function declared with any other parameters is refused at its declaration (`template-signature`); such a function becomes an ordinary function or takes its input as the text. (5) `Part`, `Part.Interp` and `compiler.parts` go (no hole is the language's); `compiler.text(e)` answers the raw text; amends 576 (2), (5), and 237, 355 where they read a language hole. (6) The codemod rewrites `name "…"` / `name """…"""` / `name("lit")` / `#[name("lit")]` → `` name @`…` `` / `` #[name @`…`] `` with the string's escapes resolved to raw bytes; the shapes it cannot rewrite are listed and migrated by hand (std and `conformance/` in B-136, the libraries in 165 s10 and their fronts). | `1-botopink-lang/144` B-136 · `2-libraries/165` s10 · every library declaring or calling a template | template_called_with_raw_text |
| 578 | (the maintainer, 2026-10-17) | **No `-> expr`: a template is marked by its `syntax` text parameter and returns a type, `anyvalue` or `anytype`; `anytype` is the bare `type`.** (1) `-> syntax T` does not exist (replaces it in 575 (4), 576 (4), 577 (2)). A template is a function whose first parameter — after an `syntax self: R` receiver, for a template method — is `syntax q: string`, the template text (577); it is called only as `` name @`…` ``, `` recv.name @`…` `` (a template method) or `` #[name @`…`] ``; an annotation's second `syntax` parameter is the annotated declaration (576). (2) **`anyvalue`** is a value whose type is not known until its code is built; **`anytype`** is a type not known until it is built, and is the bare `type` — the type of any type — renamed: `comptime t: anytype`, `compiler.Field<T>(…, type: anytype, …)`, `addType(name, syntax source: anytype)`; `type<T>` stays the type of `T` (575 (5)); `type(` / `type {` stay the inline type. A template returns an ordinary type `T` (the type of the code it answers), `anyvalue` (the call takes the built code's type, checked where the call stands, 426 (1)) or `anytype` (the call is a type, built). (3) **The compiler builds both**: `compiler.ast(node) -> anyvalue` — code built from the typed AST, a value —, `compiler.buildType(name, fields) -> anytype` — a type built from its `compiler.Field<unknown>[]`, the one way a type is built (625: no code from text): `compiler.record` goes (575 (3)); a derived type (`partial`, `required`, `merge`, `pick`, `omit`) hands the fields it kept — `compiler.fields(T)`, each field's name, type, default and annotations as written (423) — to `compiler.buildTypeInline` (625, 629), 307's rules (`derived-type-outside-val`, `derived-type-cycle`, `record-field-duplicate`) holding over the built type; the typed `compiler.addType(name, compiler.buildType(…))` replaces the string `addType(name, source)` (576 (14)); `compiler.custom(ast)` attaches the language server's tree (576 (4)). What a call becomes is the template's return value, a tree (575 (4)'s tree rule): any returned expression is captured and spliced as written. (4) `anyvalue` is written only as a template's return or `compiler.ast`'s answer (625), `anyvalue-outside-template` elsewhere. (5) The codemod writes `-> @Expr<T>` / `-> @ExprCustom<T>` → `-> T`, `@expr(v)` → `v`, a bare type-position `type` → `anytype`; a template reading only its text's value takes `syntax q: string` and reads `compiler.text(q)`. | 593 | 04s12-a · (the maintainer, 2026-10-11) | **(c), and a type may be a component — every form behaves and is called as a `fn` component.** (amended by 737: (2)'s "one record parameter, never a list" and the refusal of a second parameter are gone — a `fn` component takes plain parameters or one record, 529 (1), (2)) (1) A method, a lambda or a `default fn` answering `Component<R>` lowers as a top-level component: a lambda over the `RenderScope` (388) answering its tree and its children's scope, with `use provide` / `use context` / `await` in its body, marked by its own body (s23-j); calling it only makes the lambda and marks no caller. (2) A type implementing `Component<R>` is a component: its fields are its props and its `render(self) -> R` is its body — `type Menu(items: string[]) implement Component<Element> { fn render(self: Self) -> Element { val t = use context(Theme); return <ul class={t.cls}>…</ul>; } }` — written and called as `fn Menu(items: string[]) -> Component<Element>` is: `<Menu items={["a", "b"]}/>`, the same lowering `(props) => (scope) => …`, the same marks. The scope is never a field (388 (5): nothing captured; the renderer hands it at each run, a `Suspense` fill included). (3) Each has its own hooks node, and a method's or a type's node enters `decl.hooks` (277 amended: a method call followed through its receiver's type), so a decorator sees `Menu`'s hooks as it sees `fn App`'s. **(2) in detail** (the maintainer, 2026-10-12): a type implementing `Component<R>` is sugar for a `fn` component. Its fields are the parameters — defaults, optionals, `children`, generics as in a `fn`, immutable — and its `fn render(self: Self) -> Component<R>` is the body, `self` reading the fields and the type's helper methods. `X(fields…)` and `<X …/>` answer the component (the lambda `(props) => (scope) => …`), never a record, so a caller cannot tell the two forms apart. The same lowering on commonJS, erlang, beam and wasm, the same `.d.ts` (`export declare function X(props: {…}): Component<R>`), the same marks (s23-j: a `render` with `await` is asynchronous), the same `decl.hooks` for decorators (`#[page] type` and `#[page] fn` interchangeable), the same comptime (434-b). Hooks only in `render`; a helper method not answering `@Component` is an ordinary function. Refused, located: `implement Component` with no `render`; a `render` of another signature; a `RenderScope` field (388 (5)); a `use` outside `render`; `X.render()` called directly (`call the component, not its body: <X …/> or X(…)`). **(2) amended — a component's props are one record** (the maintainer, 2026-10-12): a component, `fn` or type, takes its props as one record parameter, never a list of parameters. `type MenuProps(items: string[], title: string = "Menu");` — then `fn Menu(props: MenuProps) -> Component<Element> { val t = use context(Theme); return <nav class={t.cls}><h2>{title}</h2><ul>{items.map({ i -> <li>{i}</li> })}</ul></nav>; }`, or the same component as a type: `type Menu(props: MenuProps) implement Component<Element> { fn render(self: Self) -> Component<Element> { …<h2>{self.title}</h2><ul>{self.items.map({ i -> self.item(i) })}</ul>… } fn item(self: Self, i: string) -> Element { return <li>{i}</li>; } }` (`item` does not answer `@Component`: an ordinary helper). The record's fields are in scope: bare in the `fn` body (`title`, `items`), on `self` in `render` and the helpers (`self.title`, `self.items`); `props` names the whole record. Defaults, optionals, `children` and generics are the record's, written once on `MenuProps`. A caller never builds the record: the attributes are its fields — `<Menu items={["a", "b"]}/>` (`title` taking its default), `<Menu items={["a", "b"]} title="Principal"/>`, `<Layout view={ { -> <Menu items={xs}/> } }/>` (inside a lambda prop alike) — and the compiler builds the `MenuProps` from them, checked field by field as a record construction is (a missing field, an unknown attribute, a wrong type: located on the attribute). A component with no props takes no parameter (`fn App() -> Component<Element>`). The lowering is unchanged — `(props) => (scope) => …`, `props` the record — and the `.d.ts` names it: `export declare function Menu(props: MenuProps): Component<Element>`, `MenuProps` exported beside it. Refused, located: a component with more than one parameter, or whose one parameter is not a record type (`a component takes its props as one record: type MenuProps(…); fn Menu(props: MenuProps)`). | `04-js` step 12 · `1-botopink-lang/144` B-17, B-116 · `2-libraries/150` | every_form_component |

(1) **The prelude is `primitives.bp` alone.** The types and behaviors above move into it from `builtins.d.bp` with their bodies (`Result`'s five methods, 575 (7)) and are written bare everywhere — `type Element(count: i32) implement Renderable`, `fn parse(s: string) -> Result<int, string>`, `fn load() -> Task<User>`; a local declaration shadows a prelude name, the rule `string` and `Array` already follow (the 01-checker row on a prelude variant's name). No `io.bp`, `io/mod.bp` piece, `panic.bp` or `asset.bp` is created. (3) **What the compiler does is a member of `compiler`**, never a std function wrapping it †: `typeInfo<T>(comptime t: type<T>) -> compiler.Info<T>` (575 (4)) — the record `TypeInfo<T>` is **`compiler.Info<T>`**, declared in `compiler.bp` (630) beside `compiler.Field<T>` (598, 599) —; `embed(comptime path: string) -> Asset` (519's rules, the file a tracked input of the build), `Asset` declared in `compiler.bp`; `external(comptime ..sources: Implementation[])`, the decorator, `Compiler.implement(source)` folded into it. In a run-time body each is written under `comptime`, `compiler` being the imported `Compiler` of a build body (575 (3), 579): `val nodes = comptime compiler.typeInfo(Page).meta(Graph)?.nodes ?? [];`, `val pixel = comptime compiler.embed("assets/pixel.bin");`. (4) **Every std decorator is written without the `@`**: `#[compiler.external(.Erlang("m:f($0)"), .JavaScript("…"))]` with `import {compiler} from "std"` — an annotation `#[compiler.m(…)]` calls the member `m` on the annotated declaration, which is the `compiler` of that point (575 (3)); `primitives.bp` writes it the same way, and `external.bp` goes, `Implementation` with its enums in `compiler.bp`; `#[Stage.…]` and `#[BeamMemory.…]` from the modules that own them, imported (`#[use]`, `#[future]`, `#[generator]`, `#[resultGenerator]` are no std decorators: 118's removed effect annotations, refused `effect-annotation-removed`, their eleven cells kept under `@`; corrected 2026-10-18). (6) b139-a narrows: `builtin` and `external` are no modules' functions, so `external.bp`'s cycle is gone, and with `compiler.Info` in `compiler.bp` (630) the behavior `Compiler` names `SourceLocation` and `Asset` alone; b139-b stands. | `machine-2/144` B-139 · `machine-1/144` B-140 · `machine-1/165` s10 | prelude_is_primitives |

| 332 | jsi64-a | **Big numbers, and `Json` read like Jackson's strict configuration.** (1) `bigint` is a primitive integer of any size with every integer operator and the literal suffix `n` (`123…890n`); erlang/beam use the VM's integer, commonJS `BigInt`, wasm std-wasm's `bigint` (442) — one value on the four targets (front `01-compiler/139`). (2) std's `Decimal` is the exact decimal (Java's `BigDecimal`: an unscaled `bigint` and a scale): `add`, `sub`, `mul` exact; `div` takes a scale and a rounding mode (`a.div(b, scale: 2, rounding: .HalfEven)`) and has no form without them. (3) `==` on `Decimal` compares the numeric value (`1.0 == 1.00`, as `compareTo`); the scale is kept and `toString()` writes it (`"1.00"`). (4) `Json` (the `json` library, 396) chooses numbers by the numeral, as Jackson's nodes with `USE_BIG_DECIMAL_FOR_FLOATS` on: an integer within `i64` is `Int(value: i64)`, a larger one `BigInt(value: bigint)`, one with a fraction or an exponent `Dec(value: Decimal)`, exact; `Num(value: f64)` goes; readers: `num() -> ?f64` (any number, approximate), `i64() -> ?i64`, `bigint() -> ?bigint`, `decimal() -> ?Decimal` (exact, `null` when the value does not fit — never a coercion), `isNumber()`, `isIntegral()`. Writing keeps the value exact: `Int` and `BigInt` as their digits, `Dec` as its plain text with its scale. (5) `#[validated]`'s bind reads a field by its own type, as Jackson's typed binding with `ACCEPT_FLOAT_AS_INT` and `ALLOW_COERCION_OF_SCALARS` off: `i64` exact (a fraction or an out-of-range integer is a violation at the field, never truncated), `bigint` any integer, `Decimal` any number exactly, `f64` any number rounded; a string becomes a number only under `#[coerce]` (325 step 6) | `01-compiler/139` · `02/97` step 15 · `03-bundled-libs/125` · `04-js` step 9 · the 13 files with a `case` over `Json` | bigint_decimal_strict_json |

A sibling or nested module reaches `config` only by import. | 431 | 24-a | **`@Result<T, E>` is botopink's checked error — Java's `throws`, Rust's `?` —, and an error that does not arrive as a `@Result` is converted at one boundary: the host binding (`result: true`) or an explicit `attempt`.** (1) **Checked:** `throw` and the propagating `try` need a `@Result` in some layer of the return; one code for both, `effect-try-without-fallible-channel`, its message naming `try … catch` and a binding's `result: true`; `try e catch f` and `case` handle on the spot in any function; every call that may fail writes its `try` — no implicit propagation (`val x = try f();` propagates, `val r = f();` is a plain `@Result`). `for-over-stream` / `for-await-expects-stream` are the renamed generator codes. (2) **Host exceptions:** `#[@External.JavaScript("JSON.parse($0)", result: true), @External.Erlang("json:decode($0)", result: true)] declare fn jsParse(s: string) -> @Result<Json, HostError>;` — each backend wraps the cell (a JS `try/catch`, an erlang `try … catch Class:Reason`, a rejected Promise under `@Task<@Result<…, HostError>>`), every way the host fails answered `Error(HostError(message, kind, name, detail))` (489, 490); `result: true` (490) refused unless the return is `@Result<…, HostError>` (or a `@Task` of one); without it the binding promises no raise, and one is a crash. (3) **Crashes:** `@panic` and an unannounced host raise are defects, never in a signature; `import {failure.attempt, failure.Failure} from "std"; val r = attempt({ -> plugin(x) });` answers `@Result<T, Failure>` (`Failure(message, kind)`, kind `panic` / `host` / `crash`), the one way to contain one — replacing jhonstart's `__jhTryValue` / `__jhTryTask` (414's renderer) and `testing.asserts`' `tryCatch` Amended by 489 (`HostError`'s fields) and 490 (`throws: true` is `result: true`, the only conversion; a host returning `{error, R}` included) | `01-checker` step 42 · `02/97` step 18 · backends 02–05 · `05-jhonstart/26` step 14 · `01-compiler/24` step 2 | result_is_checked_error |

At run time: on commonJS `throw e` / an `Error(e)` rejects the Promise with `e` and a success resolves with the bare value (no `{ Ok }` / `{ Error }` wrapper), so a JavaScript caller writes `try { await buscar(1) } catch (e) { … }` and the `.d.ts` names `E`; erlang and beam end the task's process with `{error, E}`, which `await` answers as `Error(e)`; on wasm 392's scheduler settles the task with the error. A host Promise that rejects enters as `Error` through 431's `throws: true`. A crash in a `@Task<T>` is a defect, contained by `attempt` (431). **Methods**, one spelling each (67): `map` and `then` (the bind) on every `@Task<T>`; on `@Task<@Result<T, E>>` also `mapError(f)` and `catch(f)` (recovering into `@Task<T>`); no `flatMap`. ## jhonstart & templates

| # | Id | Rule in force | Binds | Cell |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 186 | 49-f · 03r-ai | The stage a page renders in is a compile-time fact. Stages, in order: **comptime** (the build, where a page is prerendered) → **server** (request time) → **client** (the browser). A hook with no marker may run at any stage; a marker only restricts it, and the compiler detects and validates the `use` of a marked hook. **`#[serverOnly]`** — the server at request time (`searchParams`, cookies, headers, a per-request query): a page that reaches one is rendered per request — the definition of a dynamic page. **`#[clientOnly]`** — the browser only, legal only inside a `#[client]` component; written on a component's tag it renders that instance only on the client (278). Refused at compile time, at the `use`: a `#[serverOnly]` hook inside a `#[client]` component; a `#[clientOnly]` hook outside one; a `#[serverOnly]` hook in a page that declares itself prerendered (`ctr-l`). A page that reaches neither is prerendered. The build writes each route's kind (`routing`'s `k` blob, `pattern\|S\|D`) and rakun only reads it: `markDynamic` in jhonstart's `router.bp`, `rakun_ssr.erl`'s implicit mark and every channel between them go. The checker capability is 277's | 01-checker · 05-jhonstart/26 step 8 · 07-onze/49 step 5 · 04-rakun/22 step 4 | render_stage_compile_time_fact |
| 191 | — | jhonstart's templates accept in `{expr}` a closed list: a `string`; a number of any numeric type and a `bool`, each written as its `toString()` text, the same on every target; a component the template renders (a `@Component<Element>` value, 354); a list of such components; and `Node` (223), the name of that set. Any other type — a record, an optional (204), a function, a component of another `R` — is a comptime error at the expression | 118 step 2 · 05-jhonstart | template_hole_closed_type_list |
| 193 | — | A component's content is its default slot, read with `<Slot />` (360) — no `children` field in a component's props; the content is a `Node` (223); content inside the tag of a component that writes no default slot is a comptime error at the tag. Named slots: 360 | 118 · 05-jhonstart | component_content_is_default_slot |

(2) The props are records with defaults layered with `Type.merge` (307), as React's `HTMLAttributes` → `AnchorHTMLAttributes`: `GlobalAttrs` (`id`, `class`, `style`, `title`, `lang`, `dir`, `hidden`, `tabIndex`, …), `AriaAttrs`, and each element's own — `pub val AnchorProps = Type.merge(Type.merge(GlobalAttrs, AriaAttrs), AnchorAttrs);`. A field is `?T = null` (absent when null) or a `bool` (written bare, absent when false); a number is `i32` / `f64`, a closed set an enum (`target={.Blank}`, `type={.Email}`), the rest `string`; a native element's content is its builder's `children` field. (3) Names: a one-word attribute keeps HTML's name; a multi-word one is camelCase as in React (`tabIndex`, `readOnly`, `httpEquiv`, `acceptCharset`), the renderer writing HTML's (`tabindex`, `http-equiv`); `aria-*` are typed fields of `AriaAttrs` (`aria-label` is `ariaLabel`); `data-*` is the one open family, any suffix, a `string` value. | 338 | 08-d | **CSS is built in three layers — `css`, `styled`, emilia — none of which knows `.bpp`; jhonstart integrates them, and a `.bpp` file's style is a section of its own.** (1) **`css`**, the base for building CSS: its own repository, `botopink/css` at `repository/css` (326; std only). A reader of a stylesheet — rules, at-rules, comments, strings, `{ }` nesting — into a typed `Sheet`, its renderer, and `scope(id: string, css: string) -> @Result<string, string>`, which appends `[data-s="<id>"]` to each selector's compounds, leaves `:global(…)` alone and answers an `Error` naming the byte offset for a sheet that does not parse. Pure botopink, both targets, no host cell. (2) **`styled`**, the base for building CSS components: its own repository, `botopink/styled` at `repository/styled`, importing `css` and std. `styled "…"` — its `pub default fn`, a template function over `comptime css: @Expr<string>` — answers `@Component<Styled>` (354; alias `StyledView`; `Styled(className, rules) implement @Renderable`, the pattern of 276); `styledProperty "…"` holds declarations only (a `{`, `&` or `@` is an error at the character) and answers the record `StyledProperty`, not a component (381). Values go in `${…}` holes; Tailwind v4's CSS syntax works where it is CSS — `--spacing()`, `--alpha()`, `--theme()`, `@variant`, `@custom-variant` —; `@apply` is a `${p}` hole of a `StyledProperty` (its declarations inlined, no class of its own; 381); `@utility` is refused (a `val` or a function names a component, its parameters typed) and so is `@theme`. A component's class is std's `contentHash` over its rules, with the layer's prefix (`s_` by default; emilia's layer keeps `e_`, `contracts.md` § 4, 367); `styled` holds the layered sheet. What is computed at build and what at render is 355 / 377's. `behavior Styleable { fn toStyled(self: Self) -> StyledView; }` is `styled`'s, implemented by `StyledView`. **The theme mechanism is `styled`'s** — 300's `Theme`, `entry`, `clear`, `clearNs`, `clearAll`, `extendTheme`, the app's one `#[theme]` (358) and the cleared-breakpoint refusal —, read in a literal with `@variant md { … }` and `--theme(--breakpoint-md)`, resolved at build. (3) **emilia**, a series of components built with `styled`, run at compile time only (369): its families answer the record `StyledProperty` (381; `fn padAll(n: i32) -> StyledProperty { return styledProperty "padding: --spacing(${n});"; }`), its variants wrap them in a `styled` (`styled "&:hover { ${inner} }"`), its tokens reach a tag through `#[emilia(…)]` (369, 382, 383), and `defaultTheme()` holds Tailwind's values; its own sheet model goes (368); it imports `styled` and std, no framework (113). (4) **None of the three knows `.bpp`**, the manifest, `data-s` or jhonstart. **jhonstart** (its core, 361) holds the whole integration: the style function, found by `bpp`'s `#[style]` (361; a template function over the section's text answering a scoped `@Component<Styled>`); the scope id (module path and section line, sanitised to `[a-z0-9-]`); run-time holes as CSS variables — the rule reads `var(--s-<n>)`, the template's root element gets `style="--s-<n>: …"`, escaped —, which replaces `#[defineVars]`; the root context `StyledContext` it provides (354, 352); the sink that writes the render's one sheet (emilia's layers, then scoped styles in render order) in the head and each boundary fill; and `#[styled(comptime decl: @Decl, comptime ..items: @Expr<Styleable[]>)]` (364), taking the application's `styled` components only (369: `<button #[styled(btn)] #[emilia(.Pad.All.4)]>`). `jhonstart-emilia` and `jhonstart-styled` are deleted. (5) **A component activates a scoped style with `use`**: `use styled """.title { font-size: 2rem; }""";` in its body, or `use cardStyle;` for a style several components share; `html` reads it in the function's hooks (277) and writes `data-s="<id>"` on every element that function's template writes — a child's own template is not scoped by its parent. (6) **The manifest:** `"bpp": "jhonstart"` (361). (7) **The `.bpp` format** (212): the header starts on the first line, with no opening `---`, and ends at the first separator line — `---` (the markup follows) or `--- style ---` (the style follows, closed by a `---` line, then the markup to the end of the file); a file with no separator is markup only. One style section; an unscoped rule is written with `:global(…)`; `#[isGlobal]` and `#[defineVars]` go. Errors at the line: a first line `---`; a `--- style ---` after the markup; a second style section; an unclosed one; a style section in a project whose `"bpp"` package declares no `#[bpp.style]` function (361). The section unfolds to `use <style> """…""";` in the function's body, after the header's statements, before `return`. | 361 | (bpp) | **std's module `bpp` holds the four role annotations; jhonstart is one package; the manifest names it alone.** (1) **`libs/std/src/bpp.bp`** — `import {bpp} from "std";`, written `#[bpp.html]` — the contract between the toolchain and a framework, declaring four annotations in 302's shape — `html` (the markup's unfold target), `htmlPrelude` (the markup's prelude), `style` (the style section's unfold target), `stylePrelude` (the style section's prelude). (2) **Where they go:** `#[html]` and `#[style]` on the two template functions — `#[bpp.html] pub default fn html(comptime t: @Expr<string>) -> @ExprCustom<View>`, `#[bpp.style] pub fn style(comptime css: @Expr<string>) -> …`; a prelude is a module (270), and a module takes no annotation (315), so `#[htmlPrelude]` / `#[stylePrelude]` go on a marker `val` the prelude module declares — `#[bpp.htmlPrelude] pub val prelude = bpp.Prelude();` (356) — and that module's imports are the prelude. (3) **jhonstart is one package:** the member `jhonstart-styled` merges into the core `jhonstart` (its style function, scoped `use`, run-time holes, the `StyledContext` provider, `#[styled(…)]`, the sink); the core depends on `styled` (`styled` and `css` still know no jhonstart, 338 (4)). `Composable<T>`, the behavior a type implements; `Component<T>`, their union, the type every program writes and none declares or implements —; a component body answering a component resolves it; rakun-bpp's node child is `Component<Element>` alone; `RenderScope` becomes `Scope`, a cell over a persistent map of contexts; the hooks' state is each instance's; amends 388 (1), (2), 553 (2), 593 (2), 771, 791 (2), (3), 804 (1), (4); takes 791-d off B-162.** (1) **The names**, in std's `context` module (`Component` moves there from `primitives.bp`): `pub type Composition<T> = fn(scope: Scope) -> T | Async<T>;` · `pub behavior Composable<T> { fn compose(self: Self) -> Composition<T>; }` · `pub type Component<T> = Composition<T> | Composable<T>;` — one direction, no recursive alias; `Component` is a type used as `Result` and `Task` are (804): `implement Component<…>` is refused, naming `Composable`. (2) **The type form** (804 (1) respelled): `type Card(title: string, body: string) implement Composable<Element> { component fn compose(self: Self) -> Composition<Element> { … } }`; the `component` marker on `compose` is required — refused without it, naming `component fn compose` — because it is what makes the body the `Composition` over the scope and `use` exists only in a body so marked; 800's check compares `fn compose(self: Self) -> Composition<T>`, the marker no part of the signature; `Card(…)` answers a `Card`, a `Composable<Element>` and so a `Component<Element>`, and the `.d.ts` declares the type and its `compose` — 593 (2)'s lowering of the type into `(props) => (scope) => …` (built as a record `Card__Self` and a `component fn Card`) goes; how `compose` reads a field is 804-e's. (3) **`component fn`** is the short form: `component fn Hello(nome: string) -> Component<Element> { … }` — a call answers a `Composition<Element>` and runs nothing (791 (1)), the body being that function's over the scope; a `component` body's return is written `Component<T>` or `Composition<T>`, both the marker's (553 (2)). (4) **The body's answer** (553 (2)): a component body — a `component fn`'s, a `compose`'s — answers `T`, an `Async<T>` (it awaits, 791 (4)) or a `Component<T>`, the last resolved before the body returns (with which scope is 809-c's): `return div([…]);` and `return Card(…);` need no `use`, and `Component<Component<T>>` never arises. (5) **`T` is never a component nor async**: `Component<X>`, `Composition<X>` or `Composable<X>` whose `X` is a `Composition`, a `Composable`, a `Component` or an `Async` is refused where written. (6) **Resolving**: std's `resolve<T>(c: Component<T>, scope: Scope) -> T | Async<T>` — a `Composable` composed, the `Composition` applied to the scope, its answer handed back as it came, nothing awaited inside — is the one way a component runs: `use c` in a component body (an `await` emitted only where the hooks list marks `c`'s node, 375, 725), each child the renderer meets (testing `is Async<unknown>` while it descends, 504), the root `resolve(app, Scope.root())`; being a component still asks no `await` of its callers (661); `x.compose()` stays refused outside std's `context` module, whose `resolve` is its one caller (804 (1)); how `resolve` is typed is 809-a's, and whether a program may make or apply a `Composition` by hand is 809-b's; `is Composable<unknown>`, `is Async<unknown>` and `is fn(Scope) -> …` answer on the four targets. (7) **The tree (rakun-bpp)**: the node builders (`text`, `div`, …) are `component fn`s answering `Component<Element>`; `NodeChild = Component<Element>`, `NodeChildren = NodeChild[]`; the renderer resolves every child — each node a function, as React's elements are —, so no union is narrowed; how a ready `Element` enters a `Component<Element>` and what record holds the resolved tree is 809-d's.
